## 25. Bhanga — the cancellation rules, tested

*"Are there any cancel-out rules for the charts given"* is a better question
than it looks, and **this document had largely not answered it.** A search of
the reading returns no neecha bhanga, no Kuja dosha, no papakartari, no yoga
bhanga. Kemadruma is mentioned five times and never actually tested.

One item in that gap is substantial: **Mangal dosha had never been computed at
all**, despite three sections on marriage. It is the single question families
actually ask before a match.

Jyotisha's cancellation doctrines come in four families, and they work in
opposite directions:

| | |
|---|---|
| **A dosha is cancelled** | an affliction annulled by a counter-condition — *Kuja dosha bhanga, Kemadruma bhanga* |
| **A weakness is cancelled** | debilitation or combustion undone — *neecha bhanga, combustion exemptions* |
| **A yoga is cancelled** | a combination forms but cannot deliver — *yoga bhanga* |
| **A yoga requires affliction** | and is spoiled by strength — *the viparita raja yogas* |

Every rule below is **stated before it is applied**, so the reasoning can be
checked rather than taken. Where schools disagree, the disagreement is reported
rather than resolved.

### Two doctrines that are moot before we start

**Neecha bhanga.** The rule rescues a debilitated graha when its dispositor
sits in a kendra, or the graha exalted in that sign does, or the dispositor
aspects it. **Debilitated grahas in the rashi chart: none.** Every graha is
exalted, own, friendly, neutral or in an enemy sign — **not one is fallen.**
For a chart carrying this much load that is worth stating plainly. **There is
nothing to rescue.**

**Combustion exemption.** Combustion is annulled when the combust graha is
retrograde, in its own sign, or exalted.

| Budha, combust at **9.00°** from Surya | |
|---|---|
| retrograde? | **No** — speed +2.0609, direct |
| own sign? | **No** — in Mesha |
| exalted? | **No** |

> **No exemption applies.** The combustion of the lagna and 10th lord stands
> uncancelled — and since **no graha in this chart is retrograde**, no other
> body claims the exemption either.

### Kuja dosha — computed here for the first time

**The rule.** Mangal in the 1st, 2nd, 4th, 7th, 8th or 12th afflicts marriage,
reckoned from the **lagna**, from **Chandra**, and in many South Indian
traditions also from **Shukra**.

| Reckoned from | Mangal stands in | |
|---|---|---|
| **Lagna** | the **9th** | **clean** |
| Chandra | the 1st | **dosha** |
| Shukra | the 2nd | **dosha** |

**Present from Chandra and Shukra, absent from the lagna** — the single most
common configuration in real charts, and exactly why the doctrine is argued
about. **From the lagna, the reference most schools weight heaviest, he is
clean.**

**And then the cancellations.**

| Cancellation rule | |
|---|---|
| 1. Mangal own sign or exalted | No — Vrishabha, neutral |
| **2. conjunct or aspected by Guru or Chandra** | **YES — conjunct Chandra** |
| **3. conjunct or aspected by Shani** | **YES — conjunct** |
| 5. in a kendra from Guru | No — 12th from Guru |
| 6. partner carries the same dosha | Unknowable — not supplied |

**Two cancellations fire, and both are strong ones.** Mangal is conjunct
**Chandra — and Chandra is exalted there** — and conjunct **Shani**. Rule 2 is
the most widely accepted cancellation in the whole doctrine, and it applies
here in its strongest form: **not a mere aspect but a conjunction, with the
Moon in its exaltation sign.**

> **Kuja dosha is absent from the lagna, present from Chandra and Shukra, and
> cancelled by conjunction with an exalted Chandra and with Shani.**
>
> Note what that does *not* say. It does not say the marriage is unafflicted —
> §55 found the 7th empty, aspected only by Ketu and holding Upaketu, and none
> of that involves Mangal. **It says the specific dosha people ask about is not
> the problem in this chart.**

### Kemadruma does not form

**The rule.** Kemadruma arises when no graha occupies the 2nd or 12th from
Chandra, and none is conjunct it — isolation, and support that does not arrive.

| | Sign | Occupants |
|---|---|---|
| 2nd from Chandra | Mithuna | **Guru** |
| 12th from Chandra | Mesha | **Surya, Budha, Shukra** |
| conjunct Chandra | Vrishabha | **Mangal, Shani, Rahu** |

> **It does not form, and it is not close.** All three positions are occupied.
> The chart's most-cited yoga of isolation is comprehensively absent.

### Papakartari — none, and for a structural reason

**The rule.** A house hemmed between malefics — one in the house before and one
in the house after — is under papakartari, scissors affliction.

**No house in this chart is under papakartari.** The 9th shows a
**shubhakartari** (benefic enclosure) — Budha and Shukra before, Guru after —
but it is **qualified rather than clean, because Surya sits in the 8th too**,
so the enclosure is mixed.

> This follows directly from the stellium. **Seven grahas occupy two adjacent
> houses, so ten of the twelve houses have at least one empty neighbour and
> cannot be hemmed at all.** The concentration that weakens this chart in other
> ways protects it completely from enclosure affliction.

### Yoga bhanga — the family that actually bites here

**The rule**, and it is the least discussed of the four. A yoga can form
perfectly and still fail to deliver. The classical spoilers: its grahas stand
in a **dusthana**; a participant is **combust**; a participant is in an **enemy
sign**; its lords also rule dusthanas.

| Yoga | Located | Condition of participants |
|---|---|---|
| **Raja — Budha (1st, 10th) with Shukra (2nd, 9th)** | **8th — a dusthana** | **Budha neutral, COMBUST**; Shukra neutral |
| Amala — Guru in the 10th | 10th | Guru in **enemy** sign |
| Vimala — Surya, the 12th lord, in the 8th | 8th — a dusthana | Surya **exalted** |

> **The raja yoga is spoiled in two ways at once**: it sits in the 8th, and one
> of its two participants is combust. It is a genuine raja yoga — kendra lord
> with trikona lord, no argument — **standing in the crisis house with its
> kendra lord swallowed by the Sun.**
>
> **This is the central yoga bhanga of the chart**, and it is the structural
> reason a reading that keeps finding raja yogas keeps also finding they do not
> simply pay out.

**Amala is not cancelled but it is diminished** — Guru is in the 10th, which is
all Amala requires, but in its enemy's sign. **The yoga stands; the graha is
uncomfortable.**

### And the yoga that may be cancelled by strength

**The rule inverts everything above.** The viparita raja yogas — Harsha, Sarala,
Vimala — arise when a dusthana lord stands in a dusthana. Their whole logic is
that **a bad house's ruler, badly placed, cancels its own harm.** The yoga
therefore *depends on the lord being spoiled.*

A significant body of opinion holds that **a viparita yoga is itself cancelled
when the dusthana lord is strong** — because a graha in good condition does not
ruin the house it rules, and there is nothing for the inversion to work on.

**Vimala is formed**: the 12th lord Surya stands in the 8th. **But Surya is
exalted, vargottama, and the best-dignified graha in the chart.**

> By the formation rule it is **present**. By the affliction rule it is
> **cancelled** — an exalted 12th lord is not a spoiled one, and viparita needs
> spoilage.
>
> **This reading does not adjudicate.** It records that the chart's one
> viparita yoga rests on a graha too well placed to satisfy the doctrine that
> produces it — and notes that **§9 already found Vimala dissolving under every
> cuspal house frame**, by a completely different mechanism. **Two independent
> routes to doubting the same yoga is worth recording.**

### What the cancellation pass changes

1. **Kuja dosha** — the biggest practical result, and a favourable one. **Not
   an obstacle.**
2. **Kemadruma** — absent, comprehensively.
3. **Papakartari** — none, for a structural reason.
4. **Neecha bhanga** — does not arise; no graha is debilitated.
5. **Combustion** — **not** cancelled. The weakness of the lagna and 10th lord
   stands exactly as the reading has always described it.
6. **Yoga bhanga** — the raja yoga is spoiled twice, Amala is diminished, and
   Vimala is doubtful on the doctrine's own logic.

> **The afflictions in this chart cancel. The yogas do not.**
>
> Every classical dosha tested comes back annulled or absent; every benefic
> combination comes back qualified. That is an unusual and quite specific
> result — **a chart protected from the standard misfortunes and made to work
> for its advantages.** It is the same asymmetry the reading keeps meeting from
> every other direction.

---

## 26. The navamsa, read as a chart

§11 prints the D9 alongside the other fifteen Shodashavarga charts — a
placement list with house class and dignity. **That is everything this document
has ever done with it.** For the varga that ranks second only to the rashi
chart, and governs the spouse, dharma, the second half of life and the real
inner strength of every graha, **a table row is not enough.**

`verify_navamsa.py` reads it as a chart: its own lagna, its own house lords,
dignity gained and lost between D1 and D9, yogas forming inside it, and two
classical points the reading had never computed.

*One methodological note. Applying Parashari drishti* inside *a divisional
chart is done by many and rejected by others, on the grounds that a varga maps
dignity rather than a sky. Nothing load-bearing below depends on it.*

### The first fact, and this document has never stated it

| | |
|---|---|
| D1 lagna | **Kanya** |
| D9 lagna | **Kanya** |

> **The ascendant is vargottama.** §11 prints it in a table without remarking
> on it.

Classically, and without any strength measure: **the person is the same person
in the inner chart as in the outer one.** The self does not change shape
between what is presented and what is actually there. A vargottama lagna gives
steadiness of identity, resilience, and specifically that **the second half of
life confirms the first rather than contradicting it.**

**For a chart this full of tension between measures, it is the single most
stabilising thing in it.**

And note who sits on it: **Rahu occupies the D9 lagna** — and runs the
mahadasha to December 2040. **The current eighteen years are governed by the
graha placed on the inner self**, which is a structural reason the reading
keeps describing this period as identity-level rather than circumstantial.

### The navamsa as a chart

**D9 lagna Kanya, lord Budha — in D9 house 11, Karka, enemy sign.**

| D9 house | Sign | Lord | Lord in | Occupants | |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | Kanya | Budha | 11 | **Rahu** | self |
| 2 | Tula | Shukra | 3 | — | wealth, speech |
| 3 | Vrischika | Mangal | 7 | Shukra | effort |
| 4 | Dhanu | Guru | 6 | — | home, mother |
| 5 | Makara | Shani | 10 | Chandra | children, mind |
| 6 | Kumbha | Shani | 10 | Guru | service, illness |
| **7** | Meena | Guru | 6 | **Mangal, Ketu** | **SPOUSE** |
| 8 | Mesha | Mangal | 7 | Surya | transformation |
| 9 | Vrishabha | Shukra | 3 | — | dharma, father |
| **10** | Mithuna | Budha | 11 | **Shani** | career |
| 11 | Karka | Chandra | 5 | Budha | gains |
| 12 | Simha | Surya | 8 | — | loss, moksha |

**Compare that with §23's finding about the rashi chart**, where all twelve
lords crowd into just three houses. **In the navamsa they scatter across
seven.** The outer chart is concentrated; **the inner chart is distributed.**

### What each graha gains and loses between D1 and D9

This is the navamsa's classical job: a graha strong outside and weak inside
**promises more than it delivers.**

| Graha | D1 | D9 | |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Surya** | exalted | exalted | unchanged — **VARGOTTAMA** |
| **Chandra** | **exalted** | neutral | **weaker inside** |
| Mangal | neutral | **friend** | **stronger inside** |
| **Budha** | neutral | **enemy** | **weaker inside** |
| Guru | **enemy** | neutral | **stronger inside** |
| Shukra | neutral | neutral | unchanged |
| Shani | friend | friend | unchanged |

**Two of those four shifts change claims already in this document.**

**Chandra is exalted in D1 and only neutral in D9** — the classic
promises-more-than-it-delivers pattern, and it lands on a claim made two
sections ago. **§24 leaned on the 11th lord being exalted** as the counterweight
to a badly built 11th house. **The navamsa qualifies that.** The exaltation is
real outside and does not hold up inside, so the gains-through-elders channel
is **a genuine promise that thins on delivery.** §24 is not overturned — the
placement is still the best thing the 11th has — **but it was stated more
confidently than the navamsa supports.**

**Budha is neutral in D1 and enemy in D9.** The lagna and 10th lord is combust
outside and uncomfortable inside. **It gets worse, not better** — there is no
chart in the pair where the instrument of self is at ease.

And the two that improve matter too. **Guru rises from enemy to neutral**, so
the marriage and home significator is better inside than out. **Mangal rises
from neutral to friend** — which bears directly on the next finding.

**Vargottama, computed:** grahas — **Surya alone**; lagna — **yes**.

> A graha both exalted and vargottama is about as well-made as Parashari
> dignity gets, and this chart has exactly one. **It is the 12th lord, standing
> in the 8th. The best-built thing in the chart rules loss and sits in crisis.**

### The D9 seventh — the point of the varga

| | |
|---|---|
| D9 7th sign | Meena |
| D9 7th lord | **Guru — in D9 house 6, a dusthana** |
| D9 7th holds | **Mangal, Ketu** |

**Two malefics occupy the navamsa seventh.** §11 lists both placements and
never puts them side by side. Read as a chart they are one configuration: the
house of the spouse, in the varga of the spouse, holding the graha of severance
and the graha of friction and nothing else. **And its lord — the same Guru that
rules the D1 7th — falls in a D9 dusthana.**

**With one mitigation the dignity table supplies:** Mangal is in **Meena, a
friendly sign**, and is one of only two grahas that *gain* dignity in the
navamsa. **A well-dignified malefic in the 7th is not the same as an afflicted
one** — friction that is competent rather than chaotic. Ketu carries no dignity
either way.

**What this does not overturn:** the D1 7th is Meena at SAV 33 and Bhava rank
4; the Upapada falls in the rank-2 4th; the 8th from the Upapada is too weak to
dissolve the marriage; and Kuja dosha is cancelled (§25).

> **The outer chart says the marriage is structurally sound. The inner chart
> says it is emotionally austere.** Those are not contradictory — they are the
> two halves of what this reading has called **durable and undemonstrative**
> from the beginning. **The D9 is where the "undemonstrative" comes from, and
> until now that word was never actually sourced.**

### The navamsa carries no yoga at all

Computed within D9: **no kendra lord shares a house with a trikona lord. No
exchange.**

Set that against the rashi chart, which has both — Budha with Shukra in the
8th, and the 8th/9th parivartana. **The outer chart is full of combinations and
the inner chart is bare.**

> Classically that reads one way: **what he has is not seconded from the
> inside.** The rashi yogas are real — §25 shows exactly how they are spoiled —
> but **a chart whose navamsa confirmed its rashi chart would deliver more
> easily than this one does.**

### The 64th navamsa — never computed here

**The rule.** From the navamsa occupied by the Moon, and separately from the
lagna's navamsa, the **64th navamsa** forward is a classical sensitive point —
the **Khara**, or harsh, navamsa. Its lord, the **Khareshi**, is watched.
Counting inclusively, that is 63 navamsas on; each navamsa advances the
navamsa-sign by one, and 63 mod 12 = 3, so **it is the 4th sign from the
navamsa occupied.**

| From | Navamsa | 64th navamsa | **Khareshi** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Chandra** | Makara | Mesha | **Mangal** |
| **Lagna** | Kanya | Dhanu | **Guru** |

**A correction to the draft.** I had written that both Khareshis are the same
graha. **They are not**, and the computation says so plainly. The true version
is more pointed, because of what those two grahas rule:

> **Mangal**, the Khareshi from the **mind**, rules the **3rd and 8th** — the
> graha whose mahadasha ran ages 13.7 to 20.7, **the transformation already
> lived.**
>
> **Guru**, the Khareshi from the **body**, rules the **4th and 7th** — **the
> marriage, which is the thing ahead.**
>
> **One points at what is done. The other points at what is coming.** Neither
> is a prediction; both are markers the tradition says to watch, and they are
> watching the two things this reading has spent the most time on.

### The second half of life

The navamsa is weighted more heavily after about 35.

**Shani in the D9 tenth** is the placement that matters most for the later
decades, and it is one of the four career credentials the reading has cited
since §46. **Here is where it actually comes from**: the graha of structure
occupies the career house of the chart that governs the second half of life.
Its own mahadasha opens December 2056.

**And the D9 lagna lord is Budha, in an enemy sign.** Whatever else the second
half improves, **the instrument of self does not become easy.**

### What the deep pass adds

1. **The lagna is vargottama** — never stated, and the most stabilising fact in
   the chart.
2. **Rahu occupies the D9 lagna** and runs the mahadasha to 2040.
3. **The D9 7th holds Mangal and Ketu**, its lord in a D9 dusthana — the source
   of "undemonstrative".
4. **The navamsa contains no raja yoga and no exchange**, where the rashi chart
   has both.
5. **Chandra weakens inside**, qualifying §24's counterweight.
6. **The two Khareshis are the lords of the 8th and the 7th** — what is done,
   and what is coming.

> **The outer chart is crowded, contradictory and full of combinations. The
> inner chart is steady, bare and austere.**
>
> A vargottama lagna on an empty-handed navamsa is **a person who is exactly
> what he appears to be, holding fewer cards than the outer chart suggests.**

## 27. House nature — every scheme, assembled

The words *kendra*, *trikona*, *upachaya* and *dusthana* appear **237 times in
this document and are never once set out as a system.** They are used as
adjectives. And **two whole classification families are missing altogether** —
**maraka**, with zero mentions in the entire reading, and **panapara /
apoklima**, the succedent and cadent houses, also zero. For this chart the
second omission turns out to matter a great deal.

**There are six schemes, not one, and they overlap.** A house is never just one
thing.

| Scheme | | |
|---|---|---|
| **By angle** | kendra 1 4 7 10 · panapara 2 5 8 11 · apoklima 3 6 9 12 | strength of position |
| **By benefit** | trikona 1 5 9 · upachaya 3 6 10 11 · dusthana 6 8 12 | quality of result |
| **By purushartha** | dharma 1 5 9 · artha 2 6 10 · kama 3 7 11 · moksha 4 8 12 | which aim of life |
| **By mortality** | maraka 2 7 | classical difficulty markers |
| **By obstruction** | badhaka — the 7th, for a dual lagna | what blocks |
| **By modality** | chara / sthira / dwiswabhava, from the sign | how it moves |

### All six, applied

**Lagna Kanya — a dwiswabhava sign, so the badhaka is the 7th.**

| Ho | Sign | Modality | Angle | Benefit | Aim | Lord | Occupants |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Kanya | dwiswabhava | **kendra** | trikona | dharma | Budha | — |
| 2 | Tula | chara | panapara | — | artha | Shukra | — **maraka** |
| 3 | Vrischika | sthira | apoklima | upachaya | kama | Mangal | **Ketu** |
| 4 | Dhanu | dwiswabhava | **kendra** | — | moksha | Guru | — |
| 5 | Makara | chara | panapara | trikona | dharma | Shani | — |
| 6 | Kumbha | sthira | apoklima | **upachaya+dusthana** | artha | Shani | — |
| 7 | Meena | dwiswabhava | **kendra** | — | kama | Guru | — **maraka+badhaka** |
| 8 | Mesha | chara | panapara | dusthana | moksha | Mangal | **Surya, Budha, Shukra** |
| 9 | Vrishabha | sthira | apoklima | trikona | dharma | Shukra | **Chandra, Mangal, Shani, Rahu** |
| 10 | Mithuna | dwiswabhava | **kendra** | upachaya | artha | Budha | **Guru** |
| 11 | Karka | chara | panapara | upachaya | kama | Chandra | — |
| 12 | Simha | sthira | apoklima | dusthana | moksha | Surya | — |

### Where the nine grahas fall

| Scheme | | |
|---|---|---|
| **kendra** | **1** | Guru |
| panapara | 3 | Surya, Budha, Shukra |
| **apoklima** | **5** | Chandra, Mangal, Shani, Rahu, Ketu |
| trikona | 4 | Chandra, Mangal, Shani, Rahu |
| upachaya | 2 | Guru, Ketu |
| dusthana | 3 | Surya, Budha, Shukra |
| **maraka** | **0** | — |

**This is the result nobody has stated.**

> **One graha in a kendra. Five in the cadent houses.**

The kendras are the pillars — visible action, standing, effect on the world.
The apoklima houses are the weakest angular position in the scheme; grahas
there act **late, indirectly, and off the main axis.**

**Eight of nine grahas sit outside the kendras.** This document has noted "one
kendra occupied" exactly once, in passing, as the reason no Panchamahapurusha
yoga can form. **It never drew the consequence:**

> **He has almost no direct angular leverage on the world.**
>
> That single fact underwrites half of what the reading has found by other
> routes — responsibility without title, authority located out of sight,
> results arriving through the 8th–9th axis rather than the 10th, and
> advancement that has to be built rather than occupied.

### The purushartha split

Every graha sits in exactly one of the four aims, so this is a clean partition
of the nine.

| | Houses | | Count |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Dharma** | 1 5 9 | meaning, ethics, the father, fortune | **4** |
| Artha | 2 6 10 | resources, work, the material | **1** |
| Kama | 3 7 11 | desire, relationship, attainment | **1** |
| **Moksha** | 4 8 12 | release, endings, the interior | **3** |

> **Seven of nine grahas sit in dharma and moksha. One in artha. One in kama.**

And note *which* one is in kama: **Ketu, the graha that removes wanting, alone
in the 3rd.** §24 found that when asked about a rank ladder. Here it turns out
to be part of a much larger pattern.

> The chart is not merely thin on desire. **It is weighted seven to two toward
> meaning and release over acquisition and wanting** — and that was sitting in
> the classification the whole time.

### The overlaps, where the schemes earn their keep

| Ho | Labels | Occupants |
|---|---|---|
| **6** | apoklima · upachaya · **dusthana** · artha | empty |
| 10 | kendra · upachaya · artha | Guru |
| 8 | panapara · dusthana · moksha | Surya, Budha, Shukra |
| **9** | **apoklima · trikona** · dharma | Chandra, Mangal, Shani, Rahu |
| 3 | apoklima · upachaya · kama | Ketu |
| 1 | kendra · trikona · dharma | empty |

**The 6th is the interesting one** — apoklima, upachaya, dusthana *and* artha
at once. A house that is simultaneously a place of suffering and **a place that
improves with time**, whose aim is material. That is exactly why the reading
keeps finding the 6th doing useful work — 41 bindus, the D10 ascendant sign —
while also calling it a dusthana. **Both labels are correct and they describe
the same mechanism: adversity that compounds into competence.**

**The 9th is apoklima and trikona together**, holding four grahas: the most
fortunate class of house in the weakest angular position. **Fortune that is
real but does not act directly.**

**And the 10th is kendra and upachaya at once.** His career house is both a
pillar and a slow-builder. **It does not arrive; it accrues.**

### What each graha *is* for this lagna

House nature also determines what a graha **is**, because a graha takes its
character from what it rules. Rules stated before applying them: trikona lords
are benefic; dusthana lords malefic; lords of 3 and 11 malefic by lordship; **a
natural benefic ruling two kendras takes kendradhipati dosha** and behaves as a
malefic; the lagna lord is benefic regardless.

| Graha | Rules | | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| Surya | 12 | dusthana | malefic — dusthana lord |
| Chandra | 11 | upachaya | malefic by lordship |
| Mangal | 3, 8 | upachaya; dusthana | malefic — dusthana lord |
| **Budha** | 1, 10 | trikona+kendra; kendra | **benefic — lagna lord** *(kendra dosha contested)* |
| **Guru** | 4, 7 | **kendra; kendra** | **MALEFIC — kendradhipati dosha** |
| **Shukra** | 2, 9 | neutral; **trikona** | **benefic — trikona lord, no dosha** |
| Shani | 5, 6 | trikona; dusthana | **mixed** |

**Guru is a functional malefic for this lagna.** It rules the 4th and 7th, both
kendras, and is a natural benefic — full kendradhipati dosha. The reading
already lists this as one of Amala Yoga's six qualifications (§14), **but has
never stated it as what it is: the chart's great benefic is, by lordship,
working against him. And Guru rules his marriage and his home.**

**Shukra is the cleanest benefic he has** — one trikona, no kendra, no
dusthana, **no dosha of any kind.** It is also the Atmakaraka and carries the
best net balance in the chart.

> **For a Kanya lagna, Shukra is the graha to trust** — and this reading has
> been circling that conclusion from six directions without ever stating the
> lordship reason.

### Maraka — zero mentions until now

**The rule.** The 2nd and 7th are maraka houses and their lords are marakas.
**This reading declines longevity (§58) and continues to decline it**; what
follows is structural only, with no application to lifespan.

| | Sign | Lord | Occupants |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2nd | Tula | **Shukra** | **empty** |
| 7th | Meena | **Guru** | **empty** |

**Both maraka houses are empty.** And their lords are the two grahas just
identified as the extremes of the functional scale — **Shukra, the cleanest
benefic, and Guru, the one carrying kendradhipati dosha.**

**The 7th is also the badhaka** for a dual lagna, so it carries **maraka,
badhaka, kendra and kama simultaneously** — four labels. §24 found it empty,
aspected by nothing but Ketu, holding Upaketu. **It is the most heavily
classified and least occupied house in the chart.**

### What the census says that nothing else does

1. **One graha in a kendra, five in apoklima.** No direct angular leverage —
   the structural root of "responsibility without title".
2. **Seven of nine in dharma and moksha**, one each in artha and kama, and the
   one in kama is the graha that removes wanting.
3. **The 6th carries four labels at once** — which is why it reads as both his
   weakest class of house and his strongest measured one.
4. **Guru is a functional malefic here**, and it rules his home and his
   marriage. **Shukra takes no dosha at all.**
5. **Both maraka houses are empty**, their lords the best and worst of his
   functional grahas.

> **A chart with almost nothing on its pillars, almost nothing in its houses of
> wanting, and almost everything in the houses of meaning and release.**
>
> Which is the same sentence this reading reached from Ashtakavarga, from
> Jaimini, from the navamsa and from the dasha sequence. **The classification
> gets there with no arithmetic at all.**

## 28. The current transit, verified and read

He supplied a live transit table. **This document's discipline with supplied
data is to verify it first** — §2 did that with the birth data and found a
constant ayanamsa offset — so the same is done here before a word of
interpretation.

### Verifying it

The Moon fixes the moment: it moves about 13° a day, so a match to the
arcminute pins the time to within a couple of minutes. Searching 18–24 August
2026 for a Moon at 227.07 sidereal returns **one** answer:

> **21 August 2026, 12:42 IST.** Which is today.

| Body | Supplied | Computed | Diff | Sign |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Surya | 124.03 | 124.04 | 0.5′ | Simha |
| Chandra | 227.07 | 227.07 | 0.0′ | Vrischika |
| Mangal | 72.29 | 72.29 | 0.2′ | Mithuna |
| Budha | 117.41 | 117.41 | 0.2′ | Karka |
| Guru | 107.18 | 107.19 | 0.3′ | Karka |
| Shukra | 169.75 | 169.76 | 0.4′ | Kanya |
| Shani | 349.97 | 349.98 | 0.3′ | Meena |
| Rahu | 305.63 | 305.64 | 0.5′ | Kumbha |
| Ketu | 125.63 | 125.64 | 0.5′ | Simha |

**Worst disagreement across all nine: 0.5 arcminutes.** Uranus and Neptune
reproduce equally well. **Pluto is out by 22.8′** — too large for rounding;
different sources use different Pluto models, and it changes nothing here.
**The outer planets are not used below.** Parashari jyotisha works with nine
grahas; Uranus, Neptune and Pluto have no rulership, no aspect doctrine, no
dasha, and no place in any technique this reading applies.

**And one row does not verify.**

| | |
|---|---|
| supplied Lagna | **83.90** — Mithuna 23°54′ |
| Guntur ascendant at 12:42 IST | **219.44** — Vrischika 9.44° |

That is most of a chart apart. Working backwards: an ascendant of 83.90 occurs
at Guntur at **03:02 IST**, ten hours earlier, when the Moon would read 222.27
rather than 227.07. Solving instead for a longitude that would give that
ascendant *at this instant* returns **−65°** — the western Atlantic.

**So the ascendant row does not belong to the same moment as the planets.**
Some transit pages render a live planetary table beside an ascendant computed
for a stored location or a stale timestamp.

> **And it does not matter for anything below.** Gochara is read from the
> **natal** Moon and the **natal** lagna. The transit ascendant plays no part
> in it. **The one row that cannot be verified is the one row the technique
> does not use.**

### Where the transits fall

Natal lagna **Kanya**; natal Chandra in **Vrishabha**. Each transit weighted by
the **transiting graha's own bindus** in the sign it occupies — the
Ashtakavarga method, and the thing that separates a transit reading from a list
of positions. Threshold is 4.

| Graha | Transit sign | Dignity | From lagna | From Moon | Bindus | Gochara |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Surya | Simha | own | 12th | 4th | 4 | not favourable |
| Chandra | Vrischika | debilitated | 3rd | 7th | 4 | **favourable** |
| Mangal | Mithuna | enemy | 10th | 2nd | 4 | not favourable |
| **Budha** | Karka | enemy | 11th | 3rd | **2** | not favourable |
| **Guru** | Karka | **exalted** | **11th** | 3rd | 5 | not favourable |
| Shukra | Kanya | **debilitated** | **1st** | 5th | 5 | **favourable** |
| **Shani** | Meena | neutral | 7th | **11th** | 5 | **favourable** |
| Rahu | Kumbha | — | 6th | 10th | — | **favourable** |
| Ketu | Simha | — | 12th | 4th | — | not favourable |

**Four of nine favourable by classical gochara from the Moon.**

### The four things that actually matter today

**1. Guru is exalted, in his 11th, during its own antardasha.** Transit Guru in
**Karka — its exaltation sign** — in the **natal 11th**, on 5 bindus, while
**Rahu–Guru** runs to 31 January 2028. Three things point at the same graha at
once. §22 computed that window from the ephemeris — *"Guru in the 11th, INCOME,
Aug 2026 – Jun 2027"* — and here it is in his own data, **with an exaltation
that section never mentioned.**

**And the counter-reading, which gets equal weight.** Measured the classical
way, from the natal Moon, **Guru is in the 3rd — not one of its benefic gochara
houses.**

> From the natal **lagna**: 11th, exalted, 5 bindus — **good.**
> From the natal **Moon**: 3rd — **not good.**
>
> The two references disagree, and this reading does not get to keep only the
> favourable one. **What survives both:** it is the antardasha lord, it is
> exalted, and it is in the income house until June 2027. **Real, and
> qualified** — the same shape as everything else in this chart.

**2. Shani is in the 11th from the Moon — its single best position.** The 3rd,
6th and 11th from the Moon are the only places Saturn is benefic in gochara,
**and it is in the best of them**, on 5 bindus, sitting in the natal 7th.

> **Saturn leaves Meena for Mesha on 3 June 2027** — the 12th from his natal
> Moon — **and Sade Sati begins.**
>
> **The transit is currently in its most favourable classical position, and its
> next move is into its worst.**

§21 dated the marriage window's close to 3 June 2027 and §20 called it "the
clear window". **This is what that looks like from inside: the clear part is
running now, with about nine months left.**

**3. Budha is combust, on 2 bindus, and running the pratyantardasha.** Transit
Budha is **6.62° from the Sun** — his own table marks it — with **2 bindus, the
lowest of any transiting graha**, in an unfavourable gochara house. And the
current pratyantardasha is **Rahu–Guru–Budha, May–September 2026.**

> Budha is also his **natal lagna lord and 10th lord, combust natally too** —
> the same affliction repeating in transit over the graha that already carries
> it. **This is the weakest few weeks of the current antardasha**, and it ends
> in September. Nothing dramatic is implied; it is a reason not to read the
> last month as representative of the window.

**4. Mangal is 2.51° from his natal Guru.** Transit Mangal at 72.29 against
natal Guru at 74.80 — passing over the graha that forms Amala Yoga in his 10th,
rules his 4th and 7th, and runs the current antardasha. Mangal carries exactly
4 bindus in Mithuna — the threshold, adequate rather than strong. **A malefic
transit over the antardasha lord is a short contact, not a period**; Mangal
clears the degree within days. Worth noting because of *what* it lands on, not
because it forecasts anything.

### What the transit confirms

This is the useful part of a live chart: **it tests dated claims made earlier
from computation alone.**

| Claim | Made in | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Transit Shani in the natal 7th until 3 Jun 2027 | §21 | **confirmed** |
| Guru in the 11th, Aug 2026 – Jun 2027 | §22 | **confirmed** |
| Antardasha Rahu–Guru to Jan 2028; pratyantar Budha to Sep 2026 | §22 | **consistent** |
| Sade Sati begins 3 Jun 2027 — Shani not yet in the 12th from the Moon | §17 | **consistent** |

**Four dated claims, all made before this data arrived, all holding.**

### The honest summary of this moment

**Favourable:** Guru exalted in the income house running its own antardasha;
Shani in its best gochara position; Shukra crossing the natal lagna — **though
debilitated in Kanya, which has to be said rather than glossed**; Rahu in the
6th, an upachaya, where nodes do well.

**Not favourable:** Budha combust on 2 bindus while running the
pratyantardasha; Surya and Ketu together in the natal 12th; **five of nine
grahas outside their benefic gochara houses.**

> **This is the good stretch, and it is dated.** Saturn is in the best position
> it occupies in the whole cycle and moves to the worst on **3 June 2027**.
> Jupiter is exalted in the house of gains until it leaves Karka. **Nine
> months.**
>
> §20 said *"whatever is worth securing, secure it before then."* **The
> supplied transit chart is that advice with the clock showing.**

---

## 29. Job loss and career disruption

Never asked, and it is a different question from *"career growth"* (§46) or
*"how will earning grow"* (§22). Those ask what the career **does**. This asks
what happens when it **breaks**.

The apparatus is mostly **bhavat bhavam** — reading a house from another house:

| | |
|---|---|
| the 10th | the job and the standing themselves |
| the 10th **lord** | the instrument that carries them |
| **12th from the 10th = the 9th** | loss of career, expenditure of it |
| 8th from the 10th = the 5th | sudden upheaval of career |
| the 6th | the employment relation, disputes, rivals |
| the badhaka = the 7th | obstruction |

### The first result shapes everything else

| | Sign | Occupants |
|---|---|---|
| **the 10th** | Mithuna | **Guru** |
| the 10th lord | **Budha — in the 8th, combust** | |
| **12th from 10th = 9th** | Vrishabha | **Chandra, Mangal, Shani, Rahu** |
| 8th from 10th = 5th | Makara | empty |
| the 6th | Kumbha | empty |
| badhaka = 7th | Meena | empty |

> **Aspects on the 10th house: none. Nothing reaches it.**

§23 found the 10th is one of only two houses in this chart receiving no aspect
at all. Applied to *this* question it says something specific and rather
reassuring:

> **No malefic aspects his career house, because nothing aspects it.**
>
> The classical signatures of career destruction — Shani or Mangal or Rahu
> throwing an aspect onto the 10th, a malefic sitting in it, the 10th lord
> hemmed between malefics — **are all absent.** The 10th holds one graha and it
> is **Guru**, forming Amala Yoga.
>
> **The chart contains no structure for a career being destroyed from
> outside.**

### The vulnerability is the lord, not the house

Everything fragile about his career sits in **Budha**:

| | |
|---|---|
| combust | 9.00° from Surya, no exemption (§25) |
| placed | in the **8th** — a dusthana |
| Shadbala | **the only graha failing its minimum** |
| Dig Bala | 4.28 of 60 — lowest of any body |
| D9 | **enemy sign** — worse inside, not better (§26) |
| D10 | falls in a D10 dusthana |
| as career agent | ranks 4th in his own chart |

> **The career house is protected and unreachable. The career lord is the
> weakest graha in the chart.**
>
> So job loss here does not arrive as an external event striking his position.
> It arrives as **the instrument failing to hold** — his own footing, his own
> clarity about what he is doing, his own visibility.

**And the 12th-from-the-10th is the crowded house.** The loss-of-career house
is the **9th**, holding four grahas including **Rahu in Marana Karaka Sthana**
and Shani. That has to be read carefully rather than dramatically: the 9th is
*also* the trikona of fortune, and this reading has treated it as such
throughout. Both are true at once.

> Classically the 12th from a house is where its matters are **spent** — not
> destroyed, spent. Four grahas there says career is repeatedly **poured into
> something**: dharma, elders, teaching, belief, the father, foreign concerns.
> **The structure of a man who gives his working life away to something he
> considers larger, more than once.**
>
> And it is the same 9th where §24 found his gains come from. **Career flows
> out through the house his gains flow in through.**

### Five things arguing against serious loss

1. **Amala Yoga** — a benefic alone in the 10th gives reputation that does not
   tarnish. §25 found it diminished by an enemy sign but **not cancelled**.
2. **The 10th is unaspected** — nothing can reach it.
3. **The 6th is his strongest house by bindus (41)** — and the 6th is the house
   of the employment relation and of winning contests. **Protection in exactly
   the domain where jobs are lost:** disputes, rivals, reviews, competition for
   a role.
4. **The 10th is an upachaya** — upachaya houses improve with time and are
   strengthened rather than damaged by malefic contact. **His career house is
   the kind that recovers.**
5. **The lagna is vargottama** (§26) — identity does not collapse when
   circumstances do.

> **This is not a chart that describes career ruin. It describes a career that
> is hard to build and hard to destroy.**

### The one dated window

Two things must coincide: a dasha that activates the career apparatus, and a
transit that pressures it.

**Dasha side** — periods run by the 10th lord, the 8th lord, or Ketu:

| | | |
|---|---|---|
| **Rahu–Budha** | Dec 2030 – Jun 2033 | the 10th lord itself — combust, in the 8th |
| **Rahu–Ketu** | Jun 2033 – Jul 2034 | severance |
| Rahu–Mangal | Dec 2039 – Dec 2040 | the 8th lord |

**Transit side** — **Shani in Mithuna, the natal 10th: 31 May 2032 – 13 July
2034**, carrying **1 bindu of 8**.

> One bindu is **the weakest planet-sign cell in this chart's entire
> Ashtakavarga.** §17 found it while dating Sade Sati and read it for the Moon.
> Read for the *career* it says something sharper: **Saturn crosses his career
> house on the least support any transit in his chart ever has** — and it is
> simultaneously the third phase of Sade Sati.

**A correction to the draft.** I had written the concentration as ending June
2033. The overlap says otherwise: **Rahu–Ketu also runs entirely inside the
Shani transit**, and Ketu is the *more* relevant graha here, not the less.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Jun 2032 – Jun 2033** | Shani in the 10th **+ Rahu–Budha** — the 10th lord's own period, the chart's weakest graha |
| **Jun 2033 – Jul 2034** | Shani in the 10th **+ Rahu–Ketu** — severance, under the same one-bindu transit |

**Twenty-five months, in two phases, with the third phase of Sade Sati running
throughout.** §17 already called Rahu–Budha "the hinge" and §51 called it
identity and career reassessment — both reached before this question was asked.

> **That is the career-vulnerable stretch in this chart, and there is only
> one.** Nothing else in the visible timeline stacks three career-negative
> factors — and **the second phase is the more exposed**, because Ketu is what
> it is.

### What kind of loss — and this is the real answer

The chart is unusually clear about the mechanism, and it is not the expected
one.

**Against being removed:** nothing aspects the 10th, so no malefic can strike
the position; the 10th holds only Guru and Amala stands; the 6th — disputes and
rivals — is his strongest house by bindus; the 10th is upachaya and recovers.

**For leaving:** **Ketu is the sole occupant of the three houses of desire**
(§27), and Ketu's function is to make a thing stop mattering; §49 established
the chart's central mechanism as **setting things down**; the
12th-from-the-10th holds four grahas, so career is *spent*; and the 10th lord
sits in the **8th**, the house of endings that are not endings.

> **Job loss in this chart is overwhelmingly likely to be resignation rather
> than dismissal.**
>
> The structures that would show someone being fired are **genuinely absent,
> not merely weak**. The structures that show someone walking out of a role
> that has stopped meaning anything to him are **the strongest signatures the
> chart owns.**

**And the warning follows from that.** In **June 2032 – July 2034**, under
Saturn in the 10th on one bindu and the 10th lord's own weak period, the danger
is not that someone takes his job.

> **It is that he puts it down, at the worst possible moment, and calls it a
> decision.**

§51's timeline already carries *"do not resign here"* against a much smaller
window in December 2028. **The same advice belongs to 2032–34 and carries far
more weight there** — and this reading had never put it there.

### What this does not say

1. **No event is predicted.** Whether a job is lost is not something Jyotisha
   settles.
2. **Gaps between jobs are not ruin.** The 10th is an upachaya with an
   unblemished benefic in it. **Interruption is much better supported than
   collapse.**
3. **The 2032–34 window is six years out.** Nothing here bears on the job he
   holds now — §28 found the current transits are the favourable stretch,
   running to June 2027.
4. **The chart's standing advice applies.** The 6th outranks the 10th, so a
   change of position is his strongest move — **but a change made *from* a
   position, not from having left one.** That distinction is the whole of it.

---

## 30. Career from D1 and D9 only

**The constraint, stated plainly, because it shapes everything.** He asked for
both charts read deeply with every career concept applied, and for nothing else
brought in. So this uses **the rashi chart and the navamsa and nothing else** —
no D10, the dedicated career varga; no Ashtakavarga, Shadbala or constructed
scores (his earlier instruction, kept); no transits or dasha, which are timing
rather than career concepts.

What is left is larger than it sounds. **Four of the techniques below have
never been applied in this document at all.**

### Three career references, not one

Parashara reads a house from the lagna, from **Chandra** and from **Surya**.
Most readings use only the first. **This document has used only the first.**

| Reference | 10th from it | Lord | Natal house | Occupants |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| the **lagna** | Mithuna | **Budha** | 10th | Guru |
| **Chandra** — the mind | Kumbha | **Shani** | 6th | empty |
| **Surya** — the soul | Makara | **Shani** | 5th | empty |

> **Budha is the career lord from the lagna only. Shani is the career lord from
> both Chandra and Surya.**

That is the whole of an argument the reading kept running into — §21 found the
10th lord ranks fourth as a career agent behind Shani, §23 found the same from
placement, §29 found the career vulnerability is entirely in Budha. **All of it
follows from one placement fact, with no score anywhere:**

> His **nominal** profession is Budha's — analysis, communication, commerce.
> His **actual** working life belongs to Shani: structure, service, endurance,
> time.

### The 10th house in D1, and what intervenes in it

| | |
|---|---|
| sign | Mithuna — dwiswabhava, air |
| lord | **Budha, in the 8th**, neutral, combust |
| occupants | **Guru** |
| **aspects on it** | **none** |
| class | kendra + upachaya + artha |

**Argala** — never computed here. Grahas in the 2nd, 4th and 11th from a house
intervene in its affairs; each has a specific counter-house whose occupants
obstruct it (the 12th counters the 2nd, the 10th the 4th, the 3rd the 11th).

| Argala from | = house | Occupants | Counter | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2nd | 11th | empty | 9th | no argala |
| 4th | 1st | empty | 7th | no argala |
| **11th** | **8th** | **Surya, Budha, Shukra** | **12th — empty** | **UNOBSTRUCTED** |

> **One argala exists and it is unopposed.** Three grahas in the 8th intervene
> in his career, and the house that would block them is empty.
>
> Everything that acts on his career acts **through the 8th house** — crisis,
> research, other people's resources, what is hidden, what is inherited. **Not**
> through the 11th of networks, which is empty; **not** through the 1st of
> self-assertion, also empty.
>
> **The career is reached by exactly one route, and that route is wide open.**

### The 10th lord, in full

Budha at 10°27′ Mesha — the **8th house**, neutral dignity, **combust at 9.00°
with no exemption**, ruling the 1st and 10th, sitting with Surya and Shukra.
Nakshatra **Ashwini pada 4, lord Ketu.**

**The payout channel is the thing to notice.** A graha works in its sign lord's
field but is *paid out* by its nakshatra lord.

> **The lord of his career is paid out by a shadow graha.** Ketu owns no sign,
> has no substance, and its function is severance. **The 10th lord's results
> are delivered through the one body that specialises in things ceasing to
> matter** — exactly the mechanism §29 found for job loss, reached here by a
> different route using only the nakshatra table.

### The Arudha of the 10th — never computed here

Count from the house to its lord, then the same distance again. The result is
not what the house *is* but **what it looks like from outside.** The document
computed the Arudha Lagna and the Upapada and never A10.

> from the 10th (Mithuna) to its lord in Mesha = **11 signs**
> the same 11 again from Mesha = **Kumbha**
>
> **A10 = Kumbha — his natal 6th house.**

And set that beside the first table:

| | |
|---|---|
| the 10th from **Chandra** | **Kumbha** |
| the **Arudha** of the 10th | **Kumbha** |

**The same sign, by two completely unrelated techniques** — one a house-count
from the Moon, the other a reflection formula from the lord.

> **What his career is:** the 10th, ruled by a combust Budha in the 8th.
> **What his career looks like:** the 6th — **the man who does the work.**
>
> The reading has said "responsibility without title" and "the authority of the
> expert" from four other directions. **This is the placement reason, and it
> needed no strength measure at all.**

### The same apparatus in the navamsa

| | |
|---|---|
| D9 lagna | Kanya — **vargottama** |
| D9 10th sign | **Mithuna — the same sign as D1** |
| D9 10th lord | Budha, in D9 house 11, **enemy** |
| D9 10th holds | **Shani** |
| aspects on it | **Guru, Mangal** |

**The navamsa gives the career house the same sign as the rashi chart.** Not
vargottama in the technical sense — that applies to a graha or the lagna — but
the same structural statement: **the profession does not change shape between
the outer life and the inner one.**

**And Shani occupies it.** In D1 the 10th holds Guru and Shani is absent; in D9
the 10th holds Shani and Guru is absent.

> **D1 says the career is Guru's:** reputation, counsel, standing, Amala.
> **D9 says the career is Shani's:** structure, service, endurance, time.

**Where the rashi 10th lord goes in the navamsa.** A graha's navamsa is read as
the *fate* of what it rules. Budha goes from a dusthana in D1 to an **enemy
sign** in D9.

> **It does not recover inside.** §26 found this for Budha generally; applied
> to the career it says the profession never becomes comfortable. **Not that it
> fails — that it never stops costing effort.**

By contrast: **Shani holds friendly dignity in both charts and occupies the
navamsa 10th.** Guru improves from enemy to neutral but **leaves the career
house.**

### The career karakas across both charts

| Karaka | D1 house | D1 | D9 house | D9 | |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Surya** | 8th | **exalted** | 8th | **exalted** | authority, the soul of the work |
| **Shani** | 9th | friend | **10th** | friend | karma, labour, service, duration |
| Budha | 8th | neutral | 11th | **enemy** | commerce, skill |
| Guru | **10th** | **enemy** | 6th | neutral | counsel, teaching |
| Mangal | 9th | neutral | 7th | friend | technical force, contest |

> **Surya is the karaka of authority, exalted in both charts — the only graha
> that is — and it stands in the 8th in both.** As exact a statement of
> "authority that is real and not visible" as the two charts can produce.

### What the two charts agree and disagree on

**Agreed.** The career house is Mithuna in both. Budha, its lord, is
compromised in both. Surya, the karaka of authority, is exalted and in the 8th
in both. The career sits on an 8th-house axis in both.

**Disagreed.** D1 puts Guru in the 10th and forms Amala; D9 puts Shani there
instead.

**And one place where the computation corrected my draft.** I had written that
the D9 10th is unaspected like the D1 10th. **It is not:**

| | |
|---|---|
| **D1 10th** | aspected by **nothing** |
| **D9 10th** | aspected by **Guru** (its 5th, from the D9 6th) and **Mangal** (its 4th, from the D9 7th) |

> The career house is **sealed in the outer chart and contacted in the inner
> one** — and one of the two contacts is the great benefic. **Guru leaves the
> career house in the navamsa but keeps aspecting it.** Mangal, the other
> contact, is in a friendly sign there — friction that is competent.

**The three-reference test breaks the tie.** Shani rules the 10th from Chandra
and from Surya; Budha rules it only from the lagna; Shani occupies the navamsa
10th. **Guru holds one position out of four.**

> **The synthesis, using nothing but these two charts:**
>
> His profession is **nominally Budha's and actually Shani's**.
> It is reached **only through the 8th house**, by an argala nothing blocks.
> It is **perceived as the 6th** — the man who does the work, not the man who
> holds the post.
> Its lord is **paid out by Ketu**, so results arrive and then stop mattering.
> And **Amala exists in the rashi chart only** — though Guru still *aspects*
> the career house from inside the navamsa.
>
> **That last line is worth sitting with.** The yoga of spotless reputation is
> an outer-chart fact. Inside, Guru no longer occupies the career house; it
> only looks at it. **He will be well thought of, and from the inside it will
> feel like something observed rather than something held.**

### What the constraint cost

1. **The D10 is the dedicated career varga and it is excluded.** Its ascendant
   is **Kumbha — the same sign A10 and the 10th-from-Chandra both returned** —
   so it would have agreed rather than added. That agreement is worth knowing
   and is not used above.
2. **No timing.** Nothing here dates anything; the windows are in §22, §28 and
   §29.
3. **No strength measures.** Everything above is placement, lordship, aspect,
   dignity, nakshatra and arudha. **A reading that needs its own scoring to work
   is not a reading — and this one did not need it.**

## 31. Provenance — the reading mapped to Parashara

Every technique in this document has been applied from working knowledge and
verified by computation. **None of it has been checked against the source
text.** That is a real weakness in a reading that has otherwise insisted on
verifying everything, and it was raised directly: *rewrite the whole analysis
using reference books.*

**What was actually available.** Fetching primary texts is blocked in this
environment — `archive.org`, `sanskritdocuments.org`, `wisdomlib.org` and the
archive CDN all refuse the connection, by curl and by fetch alike. What
arrived instead was **the table of contents of R. Santhanam's translation of
the Brihat Parashara Hora Shastra — Volume 2, chapters 46–96, and the tail of
Volume 1, chapters 42–45** — supplied directly. Between them they fix the
volume split at 45/46.

**So this section states exactly what it is.** A **chapter-level** map from the
reading's techniques to the BPHS chapters that govern them, built from a real
table of contents rather than from recall. **It is not verse citation.** No
verse of BPHS has been read, and nothing below quotes one. What it gives is
something the document did not have at all: **an address for each technique, so
a reader with the book can check it.**

### What Volume 2 covers, and where this reading uses it

| BPHS Vol 2 | Chapter subject | Used in this reading for |
|---|---|---|
| **46** | Dasas of planets — Vimshottari and eighteen others | The whole timeline; §17 |
| 47 | Effects of dasas generally | §18, §19 |
| **48** | **Dasas of the lords of each house** — with special treatment of the 6th, 8th and 12th lords | **§18–19 read the Mangal mahadasha as the 8th lord's period. This chapter is the doctrine for that, and has not been consulted.** |
| 49, 64, 65 | Kalachakra dasa and its antardasas | **Not used anywhere** |
| **50** | Chara dasa; **badhaka houses for movable, fixed and dual rasis** | §27 took the badhaka as the 7th for a dual lagna |
| 51 | Working out antardasas | §17 |
| 52–60 | Antardasa effects under each mahadasha lord | §22, §29 |
| **61** | **Pratyantar dasas** | §22 and §28 date the current sub-sub-period |
| 62, 63 | Sookshma and Prana dasas | **Computed in §35**, then declared out of scope — a prana runs 63 hours and the birth time carries a minute of residual |
| **66** | **Ashtakavarga construction** — bindus and rekhas | §8 |
| **67** | **Trikona sodhana** | §8's reduced Ashtakavarga |
| **68** | **Ekadhipatya sodhana** | §8's reduced Ashtakavarga |
| **69** | **Pinda sadhana** | **Shodhya Pinda — used as a delivery measure throughout §19 and §22** |
| 70 | Effects of each planet's Ashtakavarga | §8 |
| 71 | Longevity through Ashtakavarga | **Declined — see §58** |
| **72** | **Sarvashtakavarga, and the rekha thresholds per rasi** | **§22 and §28 judge transits by bindu counts. This chapter sets the classical thresholds and has not been checked.** |
| 73 | Rays of the planets | **Computed in §35**, with its scaling assumption labelled, and supporting nothing |
| **74** | **Sudarshana Chakra** — the twelve houses read from lagna, Moon and Sun | **§30's "three career references" is this technique. The reading reached it independently; this is its address.** |
| **75** | **Panchamahapurusha yogas** | §21 asserts none can form |
| 76, 77 | Elements and gunas | **Computed in §35** — and both turn out to restate the stellium |
| 78 | Lost horoscopy | Not applicable |
| **79** | **Yogas leading to asceticism** — combinations for entering holy orders *and relinquishing them* | **See below. This is the gap.** |
| 80 | Female horoscopy | Not applicable |
| 81–83 | Body features, moles, curses from past births | **Not used** |
| 84–96 | Remedial measures | §57 derives remedy from the chart rather than from these. **Corrected in §35: chapters 85–94 also *define* the inauspicious birth conditions, all fifteen of which are now computed and all fifteen negative** |

### Volume 1's tail — chapters 42 to 45

A second paste supplied the end of Volume 1's contents, which **fixes the
volume split: Volume 1 runs to chapter 45, Volume 2 opens at 46.**

| BPHS Vol 1 | Chapter subject | Used in this reading for |
|---|---|---|
| **42** | **Combinations for penury** | **Never applied.** §22 and §23 read wealth at length and never tested the daridra combinations |
| **43** | **Longevity** — Pindayu, Naisargayu, Amsayu, choice among them, rectifications | **Declined.** §58 refuses ayurdaya; this names precisely what was refused — three methods, not one |
| **44** | **Maraka planets** — marakas by lordship, maraka dasa, star groups, Rahu–Ketu as marakas, **the 3rd house and death**, **occupants of the 8th** | §27 computed the maraka houses for the first time and found both empty. **It did not know about the 3rd-house rule or the 8th-occupant rule** — and his 8th holds three grahas |
| **45** | **Avasthas** — Baladi, Jagradadi, **Deeptadi (nine states)**, **Lajjitadi**, **Sayanadi (eleven)**, Cheshta | **See below. The reading uses two of five.** |

### Three gaps, not one

**First: Chapter 79, *Yogas leading to asceticism*** — combinations for
entering holy orders, and for relinquishing them.

This document's central mechanism, argued across §49 at length, is that **he
sets down the things he wanted most**: Ketu as the sole occupant of the three
houses of desire, the chart weighted seven to two toward meaning and release
over acquisition, the repeated finding that he walks away rather than being
removed.

**All of that was reasoned from placement. None of it was tested against the
chapter that codifies exactly this.** §49 uses the word *renunciation* six
times and never once applies the classical sannyasa yogas.

**Second: Chapter 45 lists five avastha schemes and this reading uses two.**

| Scheme | Status here |
|---|---|
| **Baladi** — infant to dead, by degree | **Used heavily** — Mrita Chandra, Vriddha Mangal, Bala Darakaraka |
| **Jagradadi** — waking, dreaming, sleeping | **Used once** — Sushupti Guru, as one of Amala's six qualifications |
| **Deeptadi** — nine states | **Never computed** |
| **Lajjitadi** | **Never computed** |
| **Sayanadi** — eleven states | **Never computed** |

That matters more than a missing technique usually would, because **avastha
carries load-bearing claims here**: §37's rarity figure rests on *"both
luminaries exalted and both weak by avastha"* at 1 in 3,571; §21's whole
survivability argument rests on *"Shani and Guru are the only grahas in adult
avastha"*. **Both are computed from one scheme out of five.**

**Third: Chapter 42, combinations for penury** — never tested, in a reading
that has answered questions about earning, income channels, retention and a
rank ladder.

> **These are holes rather than refinements.** In each case the reading may be
> describing a textbook configuration without having opened the textbook — or
> the classical combinations may be absent from this chart entirely, which
> would qualify the relevant sections considerably. **Both outcomes matter and
> none of the three is currently known.**

### What is still not settled

The eight disputed rules this reading has flagged as school-dependent all live
in **Volume 1's chapters 1 to 41** — not in Volume 2, and not in the tail of
Volume 1 that is now in hand:

| Dispute | Where it lives |
|---|---|
| **Varga starting signs — D5, D6, D15, D18, D22, D36** | The Shodashavarga chapter, early in Vol 1 |
| **D8 and D11 construction** — reverse-engineered here | Same chapter |
| Viparita raja yoga cancelled by strength | The yoga chapters, Vol 1 |
| Kendradhipati dosha and lagna-lord exemption | Vol 1 |
| A graha ruling both a trikona and a dusthana | Vol 1 |
| Drishti applied inside a divisional chart | The drishti chapter, Vol 1 |
| Kuja dosha reckoned from Shukra | Vol 1 |
| Argala and its counter-houses | Vol 1 |

**So the two contents pages between them cover chapters 42 to 96, and every
disputed rule sits in 1 to 41.** *(§36 now prices all eight of them — five are
still unresolved, one of those five moves six divisional charts and a quarter
of Vimshopaka Bala, and one decides whether Guru is a functional benefic.)* What has arrived names what the reading does
and exposes what it never did; what would actually change a finding is still
missing. **The Shodashavarga chapter is the single most valuable page in the
book for this document** — six declined divisional charts and two
reverse-engineered ones depend on it.

### The standard this section holds itself to

1. **Nothing above is a citation.** It is a map from technique to chapter,
   derived from a genuine contents page. A reader with the book can now find
   the passage; this document still has not read it.
2. **No claim in the reading has been changed by it.** Chapter titles cannot
   confirm or refute a rule.
3. **What it does change is the reading's honesty about its own footing.**
   Until now the techniques were applied without any stated source at all.
   **Twenty-five of them now have an address, seven are marked unused, and
   three — Chapters 42, 45 and 79 — turn out to be missing from places the
   reading argues hardest.** *(§32 closed all three of those; §35 closed the
   unused set apart from Kalachakra, which stays declined.)*

---

## 32. Closing the three gaps the contents pages exposed

§31 mapped this reading to Parashara's chapters and found three places where
the document argues hard and never opened the relevant chapter. **This section
closes what can honestly be closed.**

**The standard, stated first.** The chapters themselves are still not in hand —
only their titles. So every rule below is **applied from working knowledge and
stated before it is used**, exactly as the rest of this document has always
done. That is no worse-founded than the techniques already in the reading, but
**it is not verse citation and is not presented as any.** Where a scheme needs
a formula I cannot state confidently, **it is declined rather than guessed** —
the same treatment §12 gave the six divisional charts.

### Lajjitadi — the six states, computed

**The rules.** A graha may hold more than one state at once. *Lajjita* —
ashamed — in the 5th with a node, Surya, Shani or Mangal. *Garvita* — proud —
in exaltation or moolatrikona. *Kshudita* — starved — in an enemy's sign, or
conjunct/aspected by an enemy, or conjunct Shani. *Trishita* — thirsty — in a
watery sign, aspected by a malefic and by no benefic. *Mudita* — delighted — in
a friend's sign, or with a friend or benefic. *Kshobhita* — agitated — conjunct
Surya and afflicted.

| Graha | Sign | States |
|---|---|---|
| **Surya** | Mesha | **Garvita, Kshudita**, Mudita |
| **Chandra** | Vrishabha | **Garvita, Kshudita** |
| Mangal | Vrishabha | Kshudita, Mudita |
| Budha | Mesha | Mudita |
| **Guru** | Mithuna | **Kshudita** — and nothing else |
| Shukra | Mesha | Kshudita, Mudita |
| Shani | Vrishabha | Kshudita, **Mudita** |

**One caution on the reading of this table.** Kshudita catches six of seven
grahas, because four of them sit with Shani and the rule is broad. **A state
that nearly everything holds discriminates weakly**, and the informative
results are the extremes rather than that column.

> **And the extreme is this: both luminaries are Garvita and Kshudita at once.**
> Each in exaltation — proud — and each starved: Surya by an enemy contact,
> Chandra by sitting with Shani.
>
> That is the Lajjitadi scheme saying, in its own vocabulary, **exactly what
> §37's rarity finding says in Baladi's**: the two lights are enthroned and
> underfed. **Two independent avastha schemes, the same verdict.**

### Whether the gap damaged the two claims that rest on avastha

**Claim A — §37's rarity headline**, *"both luminaries exalted and both weak by
avastha"*, at 1 in 3,571. Baladi says Surya *Bala* and Chandra *Mrita*, both
crippled; Lajjitadi says both *Garvita and Kshudita*.

> **Survives.** A second scheme reaches the same shape by a completely
> different rule set.

**Claim B — §21's survivability argument**, that *"Shani and Guru are the only
grahas in adult avastha"* and therefore carry the coming window.

| | Baladi | Lajjitadi |
|---|---|---|
| **Shani** | adult | **Mudita — delighted** |
| **Guru** | adult | **Kshudita — starved, and nothing else** |

> **Partly qualified.** Guru is the **only graha in the chart that is neither
> proud nor delighted.** §21's argument does not collapse — Shani still carries
> it — **but it was resting half on a graha this scheme says is underfed**, and
> Baladi could not see that, because it reports both simply as "adult".

### Deeptadi applied, Sayanadi declined

**Deeptadi**, as applied here, assigns nine states by dignity and affliction —
Deepta for exalted, Swastha for own sign, Mudita for a great friend, and so on
down to Khala for debilitation, with Vikala added for combustion.

> **It adds nothing the dignity table did not already give**, because as
> applied it is largely a relabelling of dignity. **That is reported rather
> than dressed up** — it was computed, and it changes nothing. A source text
> may assign these states by rules that are *not* dignity restated, in which
> case this pass is wrong and needs redoing.

**Sayanadi is declined.** It assigns one of twelve states by an arithmetic on
nakshatra, navamsa and birth particulars, with a further sub-state on top.
**I cannot state that arithmetic with confidence, so it is left out rather than
guessed at** — the same treatment §12 gave D5, D6, D15, D18, D22 and D36.

**Three of Parashara's five avastha schemes are now applied. Two remain open.**

### The sannyasa yogas — Chapter 79, tested at last

**The rules.** Four or more grahas in one house, the strongest determining the
order entered — the principal yoga. Chandra aspected by Shani and by no
benefic. The 10th lord in the 10th aspected by Shani.

| Test | Result |
|---|---|
| **Four or more in one house** | the 9th holds **4 bodies** — Chandra, Mangal, Shani, **Rahu** — of which **3 are real grahas** |
| **Chandra with Shani, no benefic** | Shani does not *aspect* the Moon — **it sits with it**; and **no benefic reaches the Moon's sign at all** |
| 10th lord in the 10th | Budha is in the 8th. **Does not form** |

**Both of the first two land on a boundary.** The four-graha yoga forms if the
node counts and fails by one if it does not. The Chandra yoga forms in its
conjunction variant and fails in its aspect variant. **Sources differ on both,
and this reading does not adjudicate.**

But note what the ambiguity itself says: **the chart sits exactly on the
threshold of the principal renunciation yoga, in the house of dharma, with the
node of severance supplying the fourth body.**

> **What §49 gets from this is not what I expected.** It argued the
> "sets things down" mechanism entirely from Ketu in the kama trikona and the
> purushartha weighting, and never tested the classical yogas. Tested now, they
> come back **on the line — twice, by two independent rules.**
>
> **That is stronger than a clean miss and weaker than a clean hit.** The chart
> is not a textbook renunciate. **It is a chart that keeps arriving at the
> threshold of the classical combination and not quite crossing it** — which is
> almost exactly what §49 described behaviourally: a man who sets things down
> repeatedly without ever making it a renunciation.

### The penury combinations — Chapter 42, tested

| Combination | Clause 1 | Clause 2 | |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1. 11th lord in a dusthana | no — Chandra is in the 9th | — | no |
| **2. lagna lord in a dusthana with a malefic** | **yes — Budha in the 8th** | **yes — with Surya** | **FORMS** |
| **3. 2nd lord in a dusthana, 11th lord afflicted** | **yes — Shukra in the 8th** | **yes — Chandra with Mangal, Shani, Rahu, Ketu** | **FORMS** |
| 4. benefics in dusthanas **and** malefics in kendras | yes — Shukra in the 8th | **no — no malefic in any kendra** | no |
| 5. 5th lord in a dusthana with a 6/8/12 lord | no — Shani is in the 9th | yes | no |

**A correction to the draft.** I first wrote that three combinations form on
their first clause and only one survives its second. **Both halves were wrong.**
Two form on the first clause, and **two survive completely** — I had not tested
combination 3's second clause at all and assumed it failed. **It does not.**

> **Two of the five penury combinations form**, and both are versions of one
> structural fact: **the lords of self and of wealth are both in the 8th**, and
> the lord of income sits under malefic weight.

**What does not form is as informative.** Combination 4 fails on something this
reading has already made much of: **no malefic occupies a kendra in this
chart** — §27 found only one graha in a kendra at all. **The classical picture
of poverty needs malefics on the pillars, and his pillars are empty.**

And the counterweights are already in the document: §23 found the 2nd house
empty and aspected by **four** bodies including its own lord and Guru; §24
found the 11th lord exalted.

> **Two penury yogas formed against a well-aspected 2nd and an exalted 11th
> lord is a tension, not a verdict** — and it is the same tension the whole
> reading keeps meeting.

### What this pass changed

1. **Lajjitadi computed** — and it independently confirms §37's rarity finding
   by a different rule set.
2. **It qualified §21.** Shani delighted, Guru starved — not two grahas alike.
3. **Deeptadi adds nothing** as applied, and that is reported.
4. **Sayanadi declined** — the construction rule is not securely known.
5. **The sannyasa yogas come back on the boundary**, twice, each turning on
   whether a node counts as a body. §49 is neither confirmed nor refuted; it is
   placed exactly where its own behavioural description put it.
6. **Two penury combinations form**, naming a weakness the reading already knew
   under a name it did not.

**Nothing here is sourced.** The chapters are still not in hand. **What has
changed is that three techniques the contents pages named are now computed and
visible, with their rules stated — and two of them moved a claim.**

---

## 33. Rahu through Dhanishtha, and the D10 lagna claim

The claim put to the chart: *people say something will happen as Rahu moves
through Dhanishtha, because his D10 ascendant is involved.*

It has three separable parts and they are **not equally sound**. The third is
methodological rather than astrological, and it decides the answer — so it is
settled before any interpretation.

### The premise holds, and it is newer than it sounds

| | |
|---|---|
| transit Rahu | **305.64 — 5°38′ Kumbha** |
| nakshatra | **Dhanishtha pada 4** |
| sign | **Kumbha — his natal 6th house** |

His own supplied transit table says the same. **But the dates matter more than
the fact:**

| | |
|---|---|
| entered Dhanishtha | **1 August 2026** — three weeks ago |
| **leaves Kumbha for Makara** | **5 December 2026** |
| leaves Dhanishtha | **10 April 2027** |

> **There are two windows, not one, and they do not end together.**

Dhanishtha straddles a sign boundary — its last two quarters in Kumbha, its
first two in Makara. **Rahu is in the Kumbha half now and drops into the Makara
half in December.**

**That distinction is the whole question**, because everything people attach to
this transit attaches to **Kumbha, not to Dhanishtha** — and the Kumbha half
has about **three months left, not eight.**

### What Kumbha is in this chart — and it is not one thing

This is why the transit is worth taking seriously at all. **Kumbha carries five
separate identifications**, arrived at in five different sections by five
different techniques:

1. the natal **6th house** — service, employment, competition
2. the **D10 ascendant** sign (§11)
3. the **Arudha of the 10th, A10** — how the career is perceived (§30)
4. the **10th from Chandra** — one of the three career references (§30)
5. the **highest Sarvashtakavarga in the chart, 41 bindus** (§8)

> **Five career-relevant identities on one sign.** No other sign in this chart
> carries more than two.

So the instinct behind the claim is sound even where its stated reason is not:
**a transit through Kumbha crosses more career signification than a transit
through any other sign he has.** And the graha crossing it is **Rahu, running
the mahadasha to December 2040.**

### But the D10 claim itself does not survive inspection

*"Rahu is transiting his D10 lagna"* can mean two different things:

- **Loose** — Rahu is in Kumbha, and Kumbha happens to be the sign the D10
  ascendant falls in.
- **Strict** — Rahu's transit position, *mapped into the D10*, lands on the D10
  ascendant.

Only the strict version would be a transit over the D10 lagna in any meaningful
sense. **So it was computed:**

| | |
|---|---|
| transit Rahu | 305.64, Kumbha |
| **mapped into the D10** | **Meena** |
| D10 ascendant | Kumbha |
| | **so it sits in D10 house 2, not the 1st** |

> **The strict claim is false.** Mapped properly into the dashamsha, transit
> Rahu is nowhere near the D10 ascendant.

The D10 divides each sign into ten, so a transiting body sweeps a whole D10
sign every three degrees — **being "on the D10 lagna" lasts about ten weeks for
Rahu and has nothing to do with Dhanishtha.**

**And the deeper objection is worse for the claim.** §26 already recorded that
applying transit and aspect logic *inside* a divisional chart is done by many
and rejected by others, on the grounds that a varga maps dignity rather than a
sky. **This reading has never run transits through a varga and does not start
now.**

> **The D10 reason people give is the weak part of the claim. The Kumbha reason
> they do not give is the strong part.**

### What the transit actually touches

Read the ordinary way — from the birth chart:

| Aspect | Falls on | Natal house | Occupants |
|---|---|---|---|
| **5th** | **Mithuna** | **10th** | **Guru** |
| 7th | Simha | 12th | empty |
| 9th | Tula | 2nd | empty |

**The first row is the finding.**

§23 and §29 established that the natal 10th is one of only two houses in this
chart receiving **no natal aspect at all** — a sealed chamber holding Guru and
reached by nothing.

> **So a transit is presently reaching a house that nothing natal reaches — and
> it is reaching natal Guru, which runs the current antardasha.**
>
> That is a far better reason to take this transit seriously than the D10
> claim, and nobody offering the D10 claim mentioned it.

**And it changes target in December.** When Rahu leaves Kumbha on 5 December:

| Aspect | Falls on | Natal house | Occupants |
|---|---|---|---|
| **5th** | Vrishabha | **9th** | **Chandra, Mangal, Shani, Rahu** |
| 7th | Karka | 11th | empty |
| **9th** | Kanya | **1st — the lagna** | empty |

> **The second half of the Dhanishtha passage is the heavier one, not the
> first.** It turns the aspect off the career house and onto the **self**, and
> onto **its own natal position.** If a claim about Dhanishtha is going to be
> made, it belongs to **December 2026 – April 2027**, not to now.

### The nakshatra echo, which is real and small

| | | |
|---|---|---|
| natal Rahu | Mrigashira pada 2 | **lord Mangal** |
| transit Rahu | Dhanishtha pada 4 | **lord Mangal** |

The transit repeats the natal condition **at the star level without repeating
it at the sign level.** Worth naming because **Mangal is the 8th lord** and ran
the mahadasha of ages 13.7 to 20.7 (§18–19): a Rahu transit through Mangal's
star, during a Rahu mahadasha, quietly re-involves the graha that governed the
transformation already lived.

**That is an echo, not an event**, and it is stated at that weight.

### The verdict

1. **The premise is correct.** Rahu is in Dhanishtha, since 1 August 2026.
2. **The stated reason is wrong.** Mapped into the D10, transit Rahu is in that
   chart's 2nd house, not its 1st — and this reading does not run transits
   through vargas at all.
3. **The real reason is better than the stated one.** Kumbha carries five
   independent career identifications, and Rahu is the mahadasha lord crossing
   it.
4. **The sharpest fact is one nobody mentioned:** transit Rahu's 5th aspect is
   currently on the natal 10th, a house nothing natal can reach.
5. **The dates are not what people imply.** The Kumbha half ends **5 December
   2026** — about three months away, not eight. The Makara half runs to **10
   April 2027** and is the heavier of the two.
6. **And nothing here predicts an event.** A transit marks a window in which
   part of the chart is under pressure. It does not say what happens in it.

> **One thing to set against all of it.** §28 computed the current gochara and
> found Rahu in the **6th from the lagna and the 10th from the Moon — both
> favourable** positions for a node by the classical table. **Whatever this
> transit is doing, the standard method calls it well placed.**
